The Brandt-Intrau-Schiemann Table of Odd Ternary Quadratic Forms

Keywords: tables, reduced, primitive, positive definite, ternary quadratic forms, three-dimensional lattices, genus

About this Catalogue of Lattices | Return to my home page

In 1958 H. Brandt and O. Intrau published a table of primitive positive-definite ternary quadratic forms of discriminants up to -1000 -- see Math. Reviews 21 #4938. Alexander Schiemann (aschi@math.uni-sb.de) has recomputed these tables and kindly allowed us to include them here.

Notation: A ternary quadratic form

      a1 x1^2     +     a2 x2^2     +     a3 x3^2     +     a4 x2 x3     +     a5 x3 x1     +     a6 x1 x2

(where a1 ... a6 are integers) is printed as

      a1 a2 a3 a4 a5 a6

The discriminant of this form is

      d = a1 a4^2 + a2 a5^2 + a3 a6^2 - a4 a5 a6 - 4 a1 a2 a3

The associated matrix A =

  [ 2a1   a6   a5 ]
  [ a6   2a2   a4 ]
  [ a5   a4   2a3 ],

has determinant -2d.

The form is positive-definite if the associated matrix is positive-definite (in which case d is negative), and primitive if the g.c.d. of the a_i is 1.

The form is called odd (or integer-matrix) if a4, a5 and a6 are all even, otherwise it is even (or integer-valued). For example, x1^2 + x2^2 + x3^2 ( or 1 1 1 0 0 0) is odd, while A_3, x1^2 + x2^2 + x3^2 + x2 x3 + x3 x1 + x1 x2 (or 1 1 1 1 1 1) is even. For a primitive odd form, the matrix A has all even entries, and (1/2)A is the Gram matrix of a lattice whose squared lengths are both even and odd. For a primitive even form, the g.c.d. of the entries of A is 1, and the lattice with Gram matrix A has only even squared lengths.

The tables give all primitive forms of discriminants up through -1000. The forms of a given discriminant are grouped according to their genus. There is one line for each form.

(The Brandt-Intrau tables include some additional information not given here, such as the number of automorphs of each form.)

THE PRESENT FILE CONTAINS THE ODD FORMS.
FOR THE EVEN FORMS, CLICK HERE.
